# Gemini prompts → use the Art Lab

The hand-written prompts that used to be here are retired. Every terrain texture, terrain sprite and portrait is now made with the **Art Lab** (file `description/29-art-pipeline.md`):

1. `npm run dev`, then open **http://localhost:5173/artlab.html** (dev only; `vite build` ships just `index.html`).
2. Pick the asset in the catalog, then follow the numbered steps:
   - **Download guide**: the game's own procedural version at 1024 × 1024. It is always Image 1.
   - **Attach**: the exact images in order, following the generation order in 29 §B.
   - **Copy prompt**: the full, self-contained prompt.
   - **Drop result**: drop Gemini's output.
   - **Save to game**: the Art Lab normalises the output (29 §C), checks it against the 29 §D thresholds, and writes `src/assets/terrain/<biome>/<name>.png` or `src/assets/portraits/<id>.png`. A failed check blocks saving and names the fix.

The prompt text is generated from `src/artlab/catalog.ts` (materials, species, portraits) by `src/artlab/prompts.ts`. To change a prompt, edit the catalog, not this file.

The reference below was produced with the Art Lab's **Export all prompts** button. It assumes the assets are made in this order, so every style anchor already exists. The live Art Lab prompt drops an optional anchor that doesn't exist yet, and renumbers the images to match. To refresh this file, export again and replace everything below the line.

---

# Art Lab — every Gemini prompt, in generation order

Generated by the Art Lab (`src/artlab/prompts.ts` from `src/artlab/catalog.ts`). Do not edit by hand: change the catalog and export again.

For each asset: download the guide from the Art Lab (`npm run dev` → http://localhost:5173/artlab.html), attach the images **in the order listed**, paste the prompt, then drop Gemini's result back into the Art Lab, which normalises it, checks it (file 29 §C/§D) and saves it.

## Step 1 — the master style

### 1. `verdant/ground` — Meadow

Save as `src/assets/terrain/verdant/ground.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for verdant/ground (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Meadow", the base ground texture that covers most of every map of the Verdant Reach biome (temperate river valleys) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #486830. The darkest details go down to about #344b23; the brightest up to about #5f893f. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
A dense, even carpet of short meadow grass, seen from straight above — you see the blade tips, never the sides of clumps.
- fine grass blades 3–7 cm (4–9 px) long, pointing in every direction, with no combed or wind-blown look
- muted mid-green overall (#486830); deeper green (#344b22) in the gaps between blades; sunlit blade tips in soft yellow-green (#6a8a40 to #8a9a48)
- soft, irregular lighter and darker patches 40 cm–1.5 m (51–192 px) across, scattered at random: slightly drier and slightly lusher grass, not light and shadow
- a few tiny three-leaf clover leaves (2–4 cm (3–5 px)), scattered
- at most 12 very small white and pale-yellow wildflowers (2–4 cm (3–5 px)), each far from the others
Overall: the baseline green of the game: calm, natural, slightly muted, never neon.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- No other images. This texture sets the MASTER STYLE for every texture and sprite in the game, so make it exemplary.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- paths or bare dirt
- rocks or pebbles
- trees, bushes or their shadows
- water or puddles
- groups of flowers
- mushrooms
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

### 6. `scar/ground` — Glassed earth

Save as `src/assets/terrain/scar/ground.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for scar/ground (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — verdant/ground.png (master style anchor)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Glassed earth", the base ground texture that covers most of every map of The Scar biome (crystal wasteland) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #5a5070. The darkest details go down to about #413a51; the brightest up to about #756892. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
Earth fused into dark glass by the Fall, seen from straight above.
- dark violet-grey ground (#5a5070) with a hard, glazed surface — glossy in places, matte in others, but never with glare
- a network of fine cracks running in random directions, dividing the glaze into irregular pieces 15–60 cm (19–77 px) across
- patches of matte ash-grey soil (#6a6470) 20–70 cm (26–90 px) across where the glaze has flaked off
- tiny violet crystal grains (#b8a0e8, 1–2 cm (1–3 px)) catching the light, scattered evenly
Overall: the baseline of the biome: cool, dark, slightly unnatural, but still ground.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is verdant/ground.png, the game's MASTER STYLE texture (Meadow, Verdant Reach). Copy ONLY its painting style, brushwork, level of detail and ground scale. Do NOT copy its content, colours or features: this is a different material in a different biome.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- large crystals
- glowing lines
- pink or magenta
- plants
- footprints
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

```text
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is verdant/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Meadow). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- shoreline
- foam or breaking waves
- reflections of sky or clouds
- fish or water plants
- deep dark areas
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

### 15. `verdant/tree_1` — Oak

Save as `src/assets/terrain/verdant/tree_1.png` (256×256 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for verdant/tree_1 (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — verdant/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: the full, round crown of a mature oak tree — "Oak", tree 1 of 6 for the Verdant Reach biome (temperate river valleys) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90° onto the top of the object. No perspective, no side view, no visible trunk or underside.
- Silhouette and footprint: the object stays exactly where Image 1 has it, at the same size, with the same outline. It is centred, and its larger dimension spans 85% of the image (870 px) — never more.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #46703a; its shadows go down to about #2b4524 and its highlights up to about #66a254, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
The full, round crown of a mature oak tree.
- built from 7 to 10 overlapping, billowing clusters of lobed leaves, each cluster lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right
- rich mid-greens (#46703a), yellow-green highlights (#7aa04a), deep green shadowed gaps (#22381c) between clusters
- a slightly irregular, natural round outline

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 40–60 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Verdant Reach's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Every part of the object is lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right: a clear sense of volume, with self-shading only.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is verdant/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Meadow). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- a trunk or branches sticking out of the crown
- roots
- a side view, perspective or a visible horizon
```

### 31. `saltglass/forest_floor` — Scrub floor

Save as `src/assets/terrain/saltglass/forest_floor.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for saltglass/forest_floor (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — saltglass/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Scrub floor", the forest-floor texture (the ground under the trees of a forest) of the Saltglass biome (salt desert) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #8a7a52. The darkest details go down to about #63583b; the brightest up to about #ab9766. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
The sandy ground under desert scrub and date palms, seen from straight above.
- sandy tan soil (#8a7a52) with darker patches of dry humus (#6a5a3a)
- fragments of dry, fallen palm fronds and leaflets 10–40 cm (13–51 px) long, sun-bleached straw (#b8a070) and brown (#7a5a32), lying flat at random angles
- dead grass litter, thin twigs and seed pods 2–8 cm (3–10 px), scattered
- a few small thorny twig fragments and date pits (1–3 cm (1–4 px))
Overall: darker and more cluttered than the hardpan, but still dry and dusty — no green carpet.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is saltglass/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Salt hardpan). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- whole palm fronds or trunks
- green grass
- salt polygons
- rocks
- shadows of trees
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

```text
KEEP EXACTLY
```

```text
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #e8dcc0. The darkest details go down to about #c8bda5; the brightest up to about #f6e9cc. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
Pale fine sand on the shore of a brine pool, seen from straight above.
- very pale cream sand (#e8dcc0) with grains too small to see one by one
- scattered thin flakes of white salt crust (#f4eee0) 3–12 cm (4–15 px) across
- soft, non-directional dimples
- a few slightly greyer, damp patches (#d4c8aa) 30–90 cm (38–115 px) across
Overall: the palest surface of the biome, between the hardpan and the salt marsh.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES

2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
```

```text
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
```

### 55. `frostmarch/forest_floor` — Spruce floor

Save as `src/assets/terrain/frostmarch/forest_floor.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for frostmarch/forest_floor (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — frostmarch/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Spruce floor", the forest-floor texture (the ground under the trees of a forest) of the Frostmarch biome (sub-arctic tundra) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #b8c4cc. The darkest details go down to about #939da3; the brightest up to about #c7d4dc. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
Trampled snow on the floor of a spruce forest, seen from straight above.
- thinner, grey-blue snow (#b8c4cc) than the open snowfield, unevenly packed
- fallen spruce needles, small twigs and cone scales 2–10 cm (3–13 px), dark brown (#4a3a2a), scattered at random
- drip pits and small hollows 5–20 cm (6–26 px) across, slightly darker
- a few patches of dark earth and moss (#5a5a4a) 15–50 cm (19–64 px) showing through the snow
Overall: greyer and busier than the snowfield, as if under a canopy.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is frostmarch/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Snowfield). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- trunks or roots
- whole branches
- footprints in a line
- light beams
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

```text
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #6a5444. The darkest details go down to about #4c3c31; the brightest up to about #836854. No other dominant colours.
- Rows: the rows of the deck stay exactly where Image 1 has them: same number, same heights, same gaps, running left-to-right.

PAINT
The deck of a heavy iron-braced timber bridge, seen from straight above — only the deck, filling the image.
- dark weathered timber planks (#6a5444) running LEFT-TO-RIGHT, in exactly the rows of Image 1: 16 rows, each 64 px tall, with the same gaps
- white frost and a little packed snow in the gaps between rows
- two dark iron straps (#3a3a3e), each about 32 px wide, running top-to-bottom across every plank exactly where Image 1 has them (at about 23% and 73% of the width), bolted to each plank with a round bolt head
- where two planks meet end to end, a thin joint with two small nail heads; joints staggered differently in every row, never lining up vertically
- grain running along each plank; a few darker, wetter boards
Overall: belongs to the cleared road; painted, never a photograph.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.
- The deck is PAINTED like the other terrain textures — simplified wood grain and clean shapes. It must not look like a photograph of real timber or stone.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. Direction: ONLY the deck rows run left-to-right, exactly as in Image 1. Joints are staggered differently in every row so that no joint line runs from top to bottom (anything Image 1 already runs top-to-bottom, such as iron straps, stays exactly as it is). Nothing else has a direction.
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is frostmarch/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Snowfield). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- railings or posts
- the ends or outline of the bridge
- water or banks
- more than the two iron straps
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- a photo-real or photographic look of wood or stone
```

## Frostmarch: Step 4 — sprites

### 63. `frostmarch/tree_1` — Snowy spruce

Save as `src/assets/terrain/frostmarch/tree_1.png` (256×256 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for frostmarch/tree_1 (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — frostmarch/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: a snow-laden spruce seen from directly above its tip — "Snowy spruce", tree 1 of 6 for the Frostmarch biome (sub-arctic tundra) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90° onto the top of the object. No perspective, no side view, no visible trunk or underside.
- Silhouette and footprint: the object stays exactly where Image 1 has it, at the same size, with the same outline. It is centred, and its larger dimension spans 85% of the image (870 px) — never more.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #2e4a3c; its shadows go down to about #1e3027 and its highlights up to about #456f5a, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
A snow-laden spruce seen from directly above its tip.
- star-like tiers of dark green needles (#2e4a3c) radiating from the centre
- about half of every branch covered in thick snow (#f0f4f8) with blue shadows (#b8c8dc)
- the tip as a small snowy point in the exact centre

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 40–60 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Frostmarch's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Every part of the object is lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right: a clear sense of volume, with self-shading only.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is frostmarch/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Snowfield). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- a trunk
- snow on the background
- a side view, perspective or a visible horizon
```

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Charred forest floor", the forest-floor texture (the ground under the trees of a forest) of the Cinder Coast biome (volcanic arc) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #2a2422. The darkest details go down to about #1e1a18; the brightest up to about #342d2a. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
The floor of a burned-out forest, seen from straight above.
- black ash and scorched dark-brown soil (#2a2422)
- charcoal fragments and burnt twigs 5–30 cm (6–38 px) long, lying at random angles, with cracked, alligator-skin char texture
- pale grey ash flakes (#5a5450) 1–4 cm (1–5 px), scattered
- at most 8 tiny, dull orange embers (#c04a1a, 1–2 cm (1–3 px)) far apart — dull, not glowing brightly
Overall: darker than the ash plain, cluttered with burnt wood.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is cinder/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Ash plain). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- whole trunks or branches
- flames or smoke
- bright glow
- green plants
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Obsidian ridge", the mountain-top texture (impassable) of the Cinder Coast biome (volcanic arc) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #2a2628. The darkest details go down to about #1d1b1c; the brightest up to about #3f393c. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
The top of an obsidian and basalt ridge, seen from straight above — hard and impassable.
- glossy black obsidian (#2a2628) in irregular crags 50 cm–1.5 m (64–192 px) across, with conchoidal (shell-shaped) fractures and sharp glassy edges
- a faint blue-grey sheen (#6a7080) on the faces turned to the top-left; deep black crevices
- grey ash (#4a4644) settled in the cracks
Overall: black and glassy, rougher and sharper than the basalt rise.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is cinder/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Ash plain). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts

- perspective cliff walls
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

### 87. `cinder/tree_1` — Charred tree

Save as `src/assets/terrain/cinder/tree_1.png` (256×256 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for cinder/tree_1 (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — cinder/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: a dead, burned tree seen from straight above — "Charred tree", tree 1 of 6 for the Cinder Coast biome (volcanic arc) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90° onto the top of the object. No perspective, no side view, no visible trunk or underside.
- Silhouette and footprint: the object stays exactly where Image 1 has it, at the same size, with the same outline. It is centred, and its larger dimension spans 85% of the image (870 px) — never more.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #2a2624; its shadows go down to about #1d1b19 and its highlights up to about #433d3a, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
A dead, burned tree seen from straight above.
- the charred top of the trunk in the centre with 5 or 6 broken, stubby limbs radiating out
- charcoal-black wood (#1e1a18) with cracked, alligator-skin char; grey ash (#6a625a) on the upper surfaces
- a few faint orange ember glows (#c04a1a) deep in the cracks

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 40–60 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Cinder Coast's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Every part of the object is lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right: a clear sense of volume, with self-shading only.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is cinder/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Ash plain). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- leaves
- flames or smoke
- a side view, perspective or a visible horizon
```

```text
- Silhouette and footprint: the same outline as Image 1, centred left-to-right, 60% of the image width (614 px) wide. Its base sits on an imaginary ground line at 94% of the image height (963 px from the top), even if Image 1's base is lower.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #6a625a; its shadows go down to about #4a453f and its highlights up to about #8f847a, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
A small tuft of 4 longer blades, two of them curving to the sides, seen from the SIDE (front view), standing upright with its base at the bottom.
- dull grey blades (#6a625a) coated with pale ash (#8a847a), darker at the base (#3a3632)
- each blade is a clean, tapering stroke with a lit left side and a shaded right side

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 22 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Cinder Coast's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Each blade is lit on its left side and shaded on its right side.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side, including below the base.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is cinder/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Ash plain). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.
- Image 3 is cinder/tuft_1.png, the first finished sprite of this family (Ash grass tuft 1). Match its rendering, brushwork, level of detail, edge quality and lighting exactly, so the set looks like one artist painted it. This one is a different variant, as described in PAINT; do not copy its shape.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- soil, roots or a ground line under the base
- a top-down view
- a top-down view
```

## Mirelands: Step 3 — the other textures of a biome

### 103. `mire/forest_floor` — Mangrove floor

Save as `src/assets/terrain/mire/forest_floor.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for mire/forest_floor (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — mire/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Mangrove floor", the forest-floor texture (the ground under the trees of a forest) of the Mirelands biome (marsh delta) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #2c3420. The darkest details go down to about #202517; the brightest up to about #374028. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
The wet mud floor of a mangrove thicket, seen from straight above.
- dark, wet peaty mud (#2c3420)
- many small mangrove breathing-roots poking up through the mud, seen from above as round knobs and short pencil tips 1–4 cm (1–5 px) across, scattered unevenly
- fallen oval leaves 3–8 cm (4–10 px) long, olive (#5a6a30) and yellow-brown (#8a7a3a), lying flat at random angles
- patches of dark moss (#3a4a26) 15–50 cm (19–64 px) across
Overall: darker and greener than the mudflat.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is mire/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Mudflat). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- trunks or large roots crossing the image
- standing water pools
- light beams
- reflections
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

### 111. `mire/tree_1` — Mangrove

Save as `src/assets/terrain/mire/tree_1.png` (256×256 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for mire/tree_1 (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — mire/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: the crown of a mangrove tree seen from straight above — "Mangrove", tree 1 of 6 for the Mirelands biome (marsh delta) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90° onto the top of the object. No perspective, no side view, no visible trunk or underside.
- Silhouette and footprint: the object stays exactly where Image 1 has it, at the same size, with the same outline. It is centred, and its larger dimension spans 85% of the image (870 px) — never more.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #3c5a2c; its shadows go down to about #25381b and its highlights up to about #578340, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
The crown of a mangrove tree seen from straight above.
- a dense, lumpy, rounded crown of glossy oval leaves
- dark glossy green (#3c5a2c) with paler leaf undersides showing at the edge (#7a9a5a)

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 40–60 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Mirelands's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Every part of the object is lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right: a clear sense of volume, with self-shading only.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is mire/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Mudflat). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- a trunk or branches sticking out of the crown
- roots
- water
- a side view, perspective or a visible horizon
```

```text
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: a small tuft of 7 blades of different heights, one carrying a small seed head, seen from the SIDE (front view), standing upright with its base at the bottom — "Reed tuft 2", grass tuft 2 of 3 for the Mirelands biome (marsh delta) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: a side (front) view at eye level, because the game stands the tuft upright on the ground and sways it in the wind.
- Silhouette and footprint: the same outline as Image 1, centred left-to-right, 60% of the image width (614 px) wide. Its base sits on an imaginary ground line at 94% of the image height (963 px from the top), even if Image 1's base is lower.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #7a8a4a; its shadows go down to about #556134 and its highlights up to about #9fb360, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
A small tuft of 7 blades of different heights, one carrying a small seed head, seen from the SIDE (front view), standing upright with its base at the bottom.
- narrow olive reed leaves (#7a8a4a); the seed head, if any, is a brown cattail head (#6a4426)
- each blade is a clean, tapering stroke with a lit left side and a shaded right side

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 22 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with Mirelands's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Each blade is lit on its left side and shaded on its right side.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side, including below the base.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is mire/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Mudflat). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.
- Image 3 is mire/tuft_1.png, the first finished sprite of this family (Reed tuft 1). Match its rendering, brushwork, level of detail, edge quality and lighting exactly, so the set looks like one artist painted it. This one is a different variant, as described in PAINT; do not copy its shape.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- soil, roots or a ground line under the base
- a top-down view
- a top-down view
```
