### 127. `scar/forest_floor` — Crystal grove floor

Save as `src/assets/terrain/scar/forest_floor.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for scar/forest_floor (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — scar/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished, seamless, tileable game texture: "Crystal grove floor", the forest-floor texture (the ground under the trees of a forest) of The Scar biome (crystal wasteland) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this texture. It already has the correct layout, colours, scale and lighting, and it is already seamless. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted surface detail — not to invent a new picture.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px, the surface filling it edge to edge.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90°. No perspective, no horizon, no tilt, no visible sides of anything.
- Scale: the image covers 8 × 8 metres of ground, so 1 metre = 128 px.
- Layout: every lighter and darker area of Image 1 stays where it is, with the same size and shape. Do not move, add, remove or resize areas.
- Colour: the average colour of the whole image stays #3c3450. The darkest details go down to about #2b253a; the brightest up to about #4a4063. No other dominant colours.
- Seamlessness: Image 1 tiles perfectly; your image must too.

PAINT
The ground between crystal growths in a crystal grove, seen from straight above.
- dark violet soil (#3c3450)
- shed crystal shards and glassy fragments 2–10 cm (3–13 px), lilac (#9a88c8) and clear, lying at random angles
- tiny faint violet glow specks (#9a6cf0) 1–2 cm (1–3 px), scattered sparsely
- small patches of glassed crust (#4a4060) 15–50 cm (19–64 px) across
Overall: darker and more cluttered than the glassed earth.

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this image is shown at 256 × 256 px and repeated side by side, so details must stay readable at a quarter of their size.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- The sun shows ONLY in the small shading of each detail: lit on its top-left side, shaded on its bottom-right side.
- The overall brightness is identical everywhere: the four corners, the four edges and the centre have the same average brightness and colour.

RULES
1. ONE continuous surface. Not a grid, collage, 2×2 preview, contact sheet, tile set or set of variations.
2. No copied regions. Do not repeat, copy, mirror or rotate any part of the image inside itself: every 128 × 128 px area must be different from every other. The game repeats the WHOLE image by placing copies side by side, so any repetition inside it shows as an obvious pattern. (A previous attempt repeated one quarter of the image 2×2 — that is wrong.)
3. Perfectly even brightness: no glare, no sun hotspot, no reflection of the sun, no light beams, no vignette, no gradient, no darker or lighter side, corner, band or stripe. (A previous attempt had glare and a gradient, which made visible stripes when tiled.)
4. No direction: the surface must look equally natural when rotated by 90°. Nothing runs mainly one way — no ruts, tracks, streaks, ripples, waves, stripes, cracks or grain lining up, and no features arranged in rows, diagonals or a grid. (A previous road attempt had directional wheel ruts — that is wrong.)
5. Even density: features are scattered at random but evenly over the whole image — no clusters, no empty areas, no single feature larger than about 15% of the image width (154 px).
6. Seamless: the left edge continues perfectly into the right edge, and the top edge into the bottom edge. Nothing is cut off differently at an edge; no edge shading.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this texture. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is scar/ground.png, this biome's finished ground texture (Glassed earth). This texture lies right next to it on the same map: match its painting style, level of detail, scale and lighting exactly. The material and colours are different, as described in PAINT.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- people, animals, vehicles, buildings or man-made objects
- standing crystals or trees
- bright glow
- pink or magenta
- light beams
- a grid, collage or 2×2 preview, or any repeated, copied or mirrored region
- glare, highlights on the whole surface, a vignette, a gradient or light beams
- anything with a direction: ruts, tracks, stripes, rows or parallel lines
```

### 135. `scar/tree_1` — Crystal cluster

Save as `src/assets/terrain/scar/tree_1.png` (256×256 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for scar/tree_1 (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)
2. Image 2 — scar/ground.png (this biome's ground)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished game sprite: a cluster of large violet crystals seen from straight above — "Crystal cluster", tree 1 of 6 for The Scar biome (crystal wasteland) in "The Fall of the Citadel", a 2D top-down real-time strategy game (grounded military sci-fi).
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels: the single object on a perfectly flat #FF00FF magenta background.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder for this sprite: it already has the correct silhouette, footprint, colours and light. Your job is to replace its flat procedural look with painted detail — not to invent a new composition.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Camera: orthographic, looking straight down at 90° onto the top of the object. No perspective, no side view, no visible trunk or underside.
- Silhouette and footprint: the object stays exactly where Image 1 has it, at the same size, with the same outline. It is centred, and its larger dimension spans 85% of the image (870 px) — never more.
- Colour: the object's average colour stays close to #7a5ac8; its shadows go down to about #493678 and its highlights up to about #b183ff, plus the accent colours named in PAINT.
- Background: flat #FF00FF everywhere outside the object, exactly as in Image 1.

PAINT
A cluster of large violet crystals seen from straight above.
- tall hexagonal crystal prisms pointing up and outward from one centre
- translucent violet (#7a5ac8) with bright lavender facets (#c0a8ff) on the top-left and dark indigo facets (#3a2a6a) on the bottom-right
- a soft inner glow in the centre, contained inside the crystal

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.
- Crisp, fine detail with soft painted edges and visible but fine brushwork; natural, slightly muted colours.
- Clean and readable when the image is shrunk: shapes and value contrast stay clear at small size.
- NOT a photograph, NOT photo-real, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon or pixel art.
- In the game this sprite is drawn only 40–60 px wide, so the silhouette and the light/dark split must read instantly; avoid fussy noise.
- It must belong with The Scar's other terrain art: same painting style, same light, same colour harmony.

LIGHT
- Sun from the top-left, 45° above the ground.
- Every part of the object is lit on its top-left and shaded on its bottom-right: a clear sense of volume, with self-shading only.
- No light falls on the background: the background stays flat #FF00FF.

RULES
1. Background: every pixel that is not the object is exactly #FF00FF (red 255, green 0, blue 255) — flat, with no gradient, texture, noise, vignette, or lighter or darker magenta.
2. No cast shadow (the game draws shadows itself), no ground, grass, soil, snow or water under or around the object, no halo, no glow, no outline and no stroke. The edge goes straight from the object's own colours to #FF00FF.
3. Nothing touches or crosses the image edge: keep at least 4% (41 px) of pure #FF00FF on every side.
4. The object must contain no magenta, pink or hot-purple, and no colour close to #FF00FF — they would be cut out with the background. Violets must be clearly BLUE-violet (like #7a5ac8 or #a088e0), never pink-violet.
5. Exactly ONE object: no second object, no floating leaves, petals, sparks, particles or debris anywhere in the background.
6. Keep Image 1's silhouette: the same outline, the same footprint, the same centre. Every variant must fill the same footprint in the game.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this sprite. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.
- Image 2 is scar/ground.png, the ground this object stands on in the game (Glassed earth). Match its painting style, lighting and colour harmony so the object sits naturally on it. Do NOT paint any of that ground into this image.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- cast shadows, drop shadows, contact shadows, a ground plane, a base, a plinth or a pot
- glow, halo, outline, stroke or a pink/magenta fringe around the object
- a second object, particles or debris
- pink or magenta
- a glow spilling onto the background
- ground
- a side view, perspective or a visible horizon
```

PAINT
One angular, freshly split rock with flat facets and sharp ridges, seen from straight above.
- glossy black obsidian (#3a3046) with conchoidal (shell-shaped) fractures and sharp glassy edges
- thin violet veins (#9a6cf0) running through it; a faint blue-violet sheen on the top-left facets

STYLE
- Semi-realistic and painterly, like the terrain and props of classic 2000s real-time strategy games. This is an original style, not a copy of any game.

### 151. `portraits/varga` — Marshal Ilsa Varga

Save as `src/assets/portraits/varga.png` (512×512 after processing).

**Attach, in this order:**

1. Image 1 — guide for portraits/varga (download it in the Art Lab, step 1)

```text
TASK
Repaint Image 1 as a finished character portrait of Marshal Ilsa Varga of The Concord Remnant, for the mission briefing and radio-dialogue screens of "The Fall of the Citadel", a grounded military sci-fi real-time strategy game set in 2071.
Output exactly ONE square image, 1024 × 1024 pixels.
Image 1 is the game's own placeholder bust for this character: it fixes the framing, pose, size and colours. Paint it as a finished, painterly realistic portrait — do not recompose it.

KEEP EXACTLY
- Canvas: square, 1024 × 1024 px.
- Framing: a head-and-shoulders bust, centred, exactly as Image 1: the centre of the head at about 42% of the image height, the head about half the image width, the shoulders running off the bottom edge. Nothing else touches an edge.
- Pose: a three-quarter view — the head turned about 20° toward the viewer's left, the eyes looking at the viewer, the shoulders square to the camera.
- Colours from the character sheet: skin #d9b49a, hair #9a9a9a, eyes #4a6a8a, uniform #1e3a6e, trim and insignia #dbe6ff.
- Backdrop: dark and plain in #16243e, with only a soft, slightly lighter glow behind the head. Ignore the faint emblem watermark in Image 1's backdrop — do not paint it.

PAINT
- Age: 58 years old.
- Face: a stern, lean, weathered woman's face with high cheekbones, fine lines at the eyes and mouth, and a pale old scar across the left cheekbone.
- Hair: grey hair pulled tightly back into a neat low bun.
- Clothes: a dark navy military beret worn level; a high-collared dark navy Custodian Corps uniform tunic with pale silver-blue piping and shoulder boards.
- Insignia: a small badge on the collar — the Concord Custody Bar: a ring split by a vertical bar, on a small shield — in #dbe6ff.
- Expression: composed, precise and unsmiling; tired eyes that miss nothing.
Who they are: A Custodian captain at the Basin on the night of the Fall. Varga held the perimeter while the Protocol ran, then carried the Concord flag out of the burning city with three hundred survivors. Stern, precise and incorruptible, she treats custody of the shards as penance for the order she obeyed.

STYLE
- Painterly realistic, like the commander briefing portraits of 2000s real-time strategy games (the C&C Generals / StarCraft II era) — an original character in an original style, not a copy of any game, film or real person.
- Confident, visible brushwork with a finished, polished face; realistic anatomy and proportions; grounded and serious, never glamorous.
- Readable small: in the game the portrait is shown at 64–128 px, so the head shape, the face and the rim light must read instantly.
- NOT a photograph, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon, anime or comic style.

LIGHT
- Key light: soft, from the top-left at 45°, lighting the left side of the face.
- Rim light: a thin, clear rim light in #3b82f6 (Concord colour) along the right edge of the face, hair and shoulders.
- Even exposure across the face: no lens flare, no harsh spotlight, no blown-out highlights.

RULES
1. The backdrop is plain and dark in #16243e: no scenery, no room, no sky, no emblem, no pattern, no props.
2. No text of any kind: no names, letters, numbers, captions, logos or watermarks.
3. No frame, border, vignette ring, rounded corners, nameplate or UI.
4. Exactly one person, alone in the frame.
5. Keep Image 1's framing: the same crop, the same head position and the same head size.

REFERENCES
- Image 1 is the GUIDE: the game's own procedural placeholder for exactly this portrait. It fixes the layout, size, outline, colours and light. Paint over it; do not redesign it.

DO NOT
- text, letters, numbers, labels, captions, signatures, logos or watermarks
- borders, frames, rounded corners, UI elements or a white margin
- a photographic or photo-real look, a 3D render look, lens blur, depth of field, film grain, noise, chromatic aberration or JPEG artefacts
- cartoon, cel-shaded, anime, pixel-art or vector-illustration styles
- a background scene, other people, hands in the frame or props
- weapons
- helmets unless described
- medals or ribbons
- real-world flags or national insignia
- a frame, nameplate or caption
```

Who they are: Halcyon's Chief Operating Director: impeccable, pragmatic, and never cruel when cruelty is unprofitable. She genuinely believes that rebuilding the Grid will save more lives than any army, and she thinks in quarters and in centuries.

STYLE
- Painterly realistic, like the commander briefing portraits of 2000s real-time strategy games (the C&C Generals / StarCraft II era) — an original character in an original style, not a copy of any game, film or real person.

- NOT a photograph, NOT a 3D render, NOT cartoon, anime or comic style.

LIGHT
- Key light: soft, from the top-left at 45°, lighting the left side of the face.
- Rim light: a thin, clear rim light in #eab308 (Halcyon colour) along the right edge of the face, hair and shoulders.
- Even exposure across the face: no lens flare, no harsh spotlight, no blown-out highlights.

RULES

- Colours from the character sheet: skin #c49070, hair #2a2420, eyes #3a2a1a, uniform #3a4a5a, trim and insignia #dbe6ff.
